erreicht durch: stated in three places; NOT enforced anywhere in code
nur SpecINVARIANT I6 — reproducibility is a measured value, recorded in the receipt
Backend, version, GPU, driver, precision, seed, timeout, resources, repo revision, evaluator digest and artifact digests all belong in the receipt. Partial precedents exist (DSS receipt, council record header, ADR-011's receipt-join spec) but no single ExecutionTransaction receipt.
docs/IKARUS_ARIADNE_MASTER_PLAN.md:228 (I6); §13.6 GPU Receipt

nur SpecINVARIANT I7 — proactivity is a capability, not a default
Ikarus acts only inside an explicit, revocable Autonomy Envelope: permitted projects, action types, time windows, budgets, contacts, data classes and approval mode. Listed in the §20.1 Definition of Done ('Autonomy Policies technisch erzwungen'). Purely specification today.
docs/IKARUS_ARIADNE_MASTER_PLAN.md:233 (I7)

nur SpecINVARIANT I8 — recovery before 'autonomy'
A mission may only be called autonomous once it is idempotently resumable, cancellable and reconstructible after a process crash. The spine ledger is the honest partial: its own docstring states the effect_key 'delivers no idempotency on its own' and that a caller whose effect is not identifiable afterwards 'gets no crash safety here and must not pretend otherwise'.
docs/IKARUS_ARIADNE_MASTER_PLAN.md:238 (I8); kill list item "'autonomous' ohne Cancel/Recover/Kill"
erreicht durch: partially realised by daedalus/spine/ledger.py's intent-before-effect + open_intents() recovery handshake and daedalus/spine/cancel.py's Windows Job Object process-tree kill
nur SpecINVARIANT I9 — CPU/deterministic references stay normative
GPU kernels and learned accelerators are validated against a simple reference: 'Performance darf die Bedeutung nicht unbemerkt ändern.' Relevant to the structcore-rs parity question too — bypasses.md §5 makes the same demand of the Rust engine (fixture-level parity or explicitly experimental; packaging must not silently select it).

erreicht durch: stated; daedalus/accelerators.py inventories hardware but no GPU kernel is validated against a CPU reference because none exists yet
nur SpecNAME Ariadne (product)
The Forest Evolution Engine: parent/inspiration sampling, mutation, exploration/exploitation, evolutionary search state. Must NOT change the root of trust or its own evaluators. ADR-009's chosen justification for the name over 'ForestEvolve': 'She finds a verifiable path through the search space instead of pretending every mutation is progress.' forest-evolve survives as a descriptive CLI verb, not a second product identity. Crew side: no Ariadne.
docs/adrs/009; master plan §10
erreicht durch: no module; only the doc and the README pointer at line 19
nur SpecNAME Cerberus — product vs crew
Deliberately shared per ADR-010 §3. Product Cerberus = policy, capabilities, egress, approvals, resource limits; must NOT rely on system prompts as protection. Crew Cerberus = security/egress reviewer, and the one that reproduced a repository deletion against an already-patched fix. The gap between the two is the single largest doc-vs-code gap in the record: everything named 'Cerberus' in the product is aspiration, while everything effective under that name is a Claude subagent.
docs/adrs/001, ADR-007, master plan §1 + §16, docs/bypasses.md (product); .claude/agents/cerberus.md, AGENT_PROTOCOL gate 4 (crew)
erreicht durch: product: nothing — no daedalus/cerberus.py; bypasses.md is the audit of what Cerberus would have to cover. crew: an opus-tier reviewer whose CRITICAL blocks with no override
nur SpecNAME Forge (product)
Worktrees, sandbox transactions, artifacts, rollback, promotion. Must NOT pass worktrees off as a security sandbox. Notable: Forge is the only unbuilt component whose NAME appears in user-facing runtime output, which makes it the most likely to be mistaken for something that exists. Crew side: no Forge.

nur SpecNAME Nemesis — product vs crew
Deliberately shared per ADR-010 §3. Product Nemesis = the independent adversarial verifier that runs BEFORE promotion and must not be controllable by the candidate (I4). Crew Nemesis = attacks the RUNNING result, constructs a repro and proves the break with a real exploit and a failing test — 'Does not review by reading', review-only, never edits. Distinguished in its own file from Momus, who attacks the plan on paper beforehand.

erreicht durch: product: nothing — ADR-004 states plainly 'Nemesis is not implemented'. crew: qa-critic is dispatched as Nemesis
nur SpecNAME Talos — product vs crew
ADR-010 §3 declares Talos DELIBERATELY SHARED: 'the crew agent is the dev-time instance of the same gate role the runtime component plays.' Product Talos = versioned evaluator packs (talos-evaluator/1 manifest, digest, required_isolation, staged hard gates, metrics). Crew Talos = the test-suite owner. DEFECT: AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:80 calls the same agent 'Týr (test-dev)' in the gate list while test-dev.md:6 says 'You are Talos' — two names for one actor inside one repo, which is exactly what ADR-010 exists to prevent.

nur SpecNAME The Grove (product)
Ariadne's append-only Quality-Diversity candidate archive with lineage and Pareto/QD niches. Must NOT keep only the current winner. Spec includes a candidate_id = SHA256(base + canonical patch + generator + mutation config), hot/warm/cold storage tiers, an ArtifactStore contract (put/open/pin/release/gc/capacity), mark-and-sweep GC over pinned lineage, and storage_unavailable rather than silent fallback to C:. Crew side: no Grove.

dunkelINVARIANT a Cerberus CRITICAL blocks, no override
Deliberate ownership split stated in the protocol: 'Minos writes the fence and Cerberus vetoes breaches of it. An owner reviewing their own work is the anti-pattern this crew exists to avoid.' dunkelINVARIANT a test that passes because the fixture is inert is worse than no test
HANDOFF records the concrete failure that promoted this from a standing order to a gate: three guards in worktree.py SURVIVED THEIR OWN DELETION — the suite stayed fully green with _refuse_if_repo_adjacent, _remove_tree_no_follow and the reap sha-proof each disabled. 'A guard whose absence no test detects is decoration.' Round 2 rule: every guard kept must have a test that goes red when that specific guard is disabled, verified by actually disabling it.

dunkelINVARIANT actor namespacing daedalus.<n> / crew.<n> / council.<vendor>.<model>
ADR-010: 'A bare Greek name in a durable record is now a defect, not a style choice.' ADR-011 §5 states the deliberate, stated consequence of not tightening memstore: 'a ledger entry written outside the spine's helper may carry a bare actor, and such an entry is not citable in a receipt. The journal is unenforced and is not citable at all.' ADR-012 adds that a bare vendor name is a defect for the same reason — the model behind a stable CLI name changes underneath it, so a dissent attributed only to 'claude' is not reproducible.

dunkelINVARIANT I2 — no silent primary-checkout write; all mutation through one verified transaction
The transaction the invariant requires (base revision + declared write scope + isolated copy + policy profile + event journal + evaluator receipt + explicit promotion) does not exist as one object. The honest form: the HARNESS never applies a patch to the primary checkout and no promotion path does; candidate containment needs an OS sandbox that does not exist.

dunkelINVARIANT I3 + I10 — an unvalidated metric never gates autonomy
I3: latent similarity, novelty, DSS relevance, a GNN or a surrogate may PRIORITIZE candidates and may never overrule a failed hard gate. I10: 'SOTA', 'diffeomorph', 'semantisch', 'conflict-free', 'self-improving', 'besser als AlphaEvolve' are result terms, not names for unfinished modules. Enforcement is editorial, not mechanical — no test rejects a metric that has not been validated.

dunkelINVARIANT measurement validity is a gate, not a habit
Earned by three wrong numbers reported as fact in one session: 1.47x speedup that was actually 0.99x (baseline measured while 23 agent processes ran), 171.0s that was 86.5s, 499 corrupted files that were 66 (measured a code path Python does not take). 'A benchmark whose validity was not checked is not evidence.'
.claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:98-120 ('Why these rules exist' #1)
erreicht durch: assigned to the crew delegate .claude/agents/metron.md, whose description says it 'Refuses to time anything while the box is under load'
dunkelINVARIANT one router, no agent-to-agent chat, the router owns the verdict
Tension worth naming: runs/council/room.py ('Der Raum') is a shared transcript in which agents from four vendors read each other's turns — the exact shape the no-freeform-chatter rule targets. It is defensible because the room has an artifact contract (hash-chained bus, per-speaker unread cursor, distilled attachments) but no document reconciles the two rules.

erreicht durch: crew process; the product side states the same rule in README ('No agent talks directly to another agent') and the master plan kill list forbids 'Multi-Agent-Freeform-Chatter ohne Artifact Contract'
dunkelINVARIANT parallel writers get isolation (never stash a shared tree)
Earned by a real failure: a git stash of clones.py during an active round left the tree transiently un-importable and a Nemesis agent independently reported it as a defect. Reinforced by master plan P0 blocker 2 — write parallelism is disabled until separate workcells exist, because parallel write tasks compared only DECLARED path strings while an agentic writer could touch undeclared paths.
.claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:138-150 (#3)
erreicht durch: worktree support exists (daedalus/kairos/worktree.py, the Agent tool's isolation:'worktree'); the rule itself is unenforced
dunkelINVARIANT provenance — MEASURED / INHERITED / ASSUMED on every number
The failure it prevents is specific and nasty: a handoff written by a PREVIOUS SESSION OF THE SAME MODEL was later cited as independent evidence with its own hedge silently dropped. 'Your own prior output is the most invisible kind of inherited context there is.' An unattributable number is ASSUMED and may not carry an argument alone.
.claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:122-136 (#2)
erreicht durch: assigned to .claude/agents/mnemosyne.md; visibly applied in docs/HANDOFF.md ('Measured, not inherited: python -m pytest -q -> 1223 passed')
dunkelINVARIANT the archive is permanent — append-only on every durable resource
The strength varies by store and the docs say so rather than papering over it: bypasses.md §3 and ADR-011 §3 both state the JSONL journal is convention-only and the projection store carries no tamper-evidence and is 'not to be given any'. ADR-011's mitigation is that the weak store is removed from every trust path. The Grove itself does not exist. Re-evaluation must create a NEW evaluation record, never overwrite (master plan §10.3), and GC is mark-and-sweep over pinned lineage, never age-based deletion.
docs/adrs/006-memory-separation.md; docs/adrs/009 ("The Grove is Ariadne's append-only ... archive"); master plan §10.3 ("Nichts wird durch einen neuen Score überschrieben")
erreicht durch: genuinely tamper-evident only in daedalus/memstore.py (dmem/1 hash chain, body_sha + entry_sha + prev + head anchor) and daedalus/council/bus.py (dcouncil/1, same two-SHA discipline); spine/ledger.py is append-only by construction (no UPDATE statement); memory/events.local.jsonl is append-only BY CONVENTION ONLY
dunkelINVARIANT the test-thermometer rule
A proposal without a NAMED, RUNNABLE measurement carrying a BEFORE reading and an EXPECTED AFTER is not a proposal. "'Run the tests' is not a thermometer; '1/21 shapes named -> 21/21' is." This is the same discipline ADR-012 §7 and EXPERIMENT_A_B apply at the design level — pre-register the falsifier.

staleKILL hyperbolic geometry as semantics
What was actually there: Euclidean embeddings RADIALLY PROJECTED into a Poincaré ball and called hyperbolic semantics. Cited prior art: Nickel & Kiela 2017, Balazevic et al. 2019.

läuftDOC ADR-011 The Event Spine
Status: Proposed. Names four durable stores and picks daedalus/spine/ledger.py (SQLite WAL) as ordering authority on CAPABILITY grounds, demotes memstore to attestation sidecar, embeddings to projection, and the JSONL journal to non-load-bearing narration. Also forbids a fifth durable store. Carries five explicit revisit triggers. NOT in FEATURE_INVENTORY.json — the largest single omission in that file's coverage of the written record.
docs/adrs/011-event-spine.md
erreicht durch: daedalus/spine/ledger.py landed after it (commit a508da5); HANDOFF.md line 63 lists it
läuftDOC ADR-012 Der Rat — Cross-Vendor Review Council
Status: Proposed, 20.9 KB, the most detailed ADR by far. Nine numbered decisions: no verdict field (structural), council.<vendor>.<model> ids, independence-class = (vendor, model_family), completions-not-agents, per-file bidirectional secret floor, dissent never averaged, degraded quorum as a first-class statement, fail-closed budgets, and council turns never entering the memory ledger. Six revisit triggers. NOT in FEATURE_INVENTORY.json.
docs/adrs/012-council-cross-vendor-review.md
erreicht durch: daedalus/council/{bus,vendors,session,publish,canary}.py implement it; .claude/skills/council/SKILL.md restates its doctrine; tests/test_council_*.py enforce five of its clauses
läuftDOC bypasses.md — known bypasses and security gaps
Audited 2026-07-28, five gaps: (1) multiple uncentralized network/provider paths; (2) filesystem and process access — 'A Git worktree isolates changes from the primary checkout; it does not isolate the host or secrets'; (3) JSONL append-only by convention, not tamper-evident; (4) Kairos is not a DAG scheduler and Ikarus has no upstream; (5) structcore-rs lags Python and must not be silently selected as an equivalent backend. Each gap carries a remediation list. This is the document that makes 'Cerberus' honest.

läuftDOC ENGINE_PARITY.md — Python vs Rust structcore
17.5 KB, self-limits in its own header: cargo was not on PATH, so no Rust code was built or run — source reading plus earlier wall-clock numbers only. Python 18 languages/33 extensions vs Rust 6/11 (strict subset). §6 records a defect SHARED by both engines (C/C++ function names resolve to <anonymous> because neither descends into declarator/function_declarator) and warns that fixing Python WIDENS the divergence. Names a sentinel mismatch (<anon> vs <anonymous>) that would leak past clones.py's filter. §7 is a priority-ordered porter list.

läuftDOC FOUNDATION_AUDIT.md
Dated 2026-07-28. The audit that produced the ADR rewrite: a ten-row verdict table naming what survived and what was removed (hyperbolic search, 'gradient' evolution, Hermes, the Tailwind/Three/Tauri prototype scaffold). Also defines 'the real forest model' (nodes/typed edge layers/hyperedges/provenance/immutable snapshots), what MetaCoding should mean as six steps with receipts, and a four-stage math roadmap where each stage carries a named gate. Cited references are real papers (Nickel & Kiela, Balazevic, KaHyPar, Interlat).

läuftDOC master plan §3.2 'noch nicht belastbar' regression list
Twelve explicit not-yet-true claims: Kairos is not a persistent DAG orchestrator; no canonical MissionSpec/durable store/crash recovery; offload path and adapter/worktree path are two different execution worlds; a worktree isolates git not the host; EvolutionaryOrchestrator is Best-of-N + pytest; evaluator and candidate not separated by a root of trust; personal memory has no consent model; latent search indexes agent events not the code forest; no hidden-state comms; RTX is not a registered worker; HEP is not a domain pack. All twelve verified still true today.

läuftDOC master plan §3.3 P0 blockers before further autonomy
Five blockers: (1) legacy forced-codex wrote the primary checkout — now advisory-only, VERIFIED in core.py:638/676 and offload.py:387; (2) parallel write tasks compared declared path strings only — write parallelism disabled; (3) memory->vector hook lost provenance — fixed, sync projection still a compat path; (4) system disk had ~0.57 GiB free on 2026-07-28; (5) the evolution runner is a fixed pytest call and stays a Best-of-N baseline, not Ariadne.

läuftINVARIANT additive endpoints only — /api/dashboard shape is frozen
One of the few standing orders with a named enforcing test rather than a reviewer.
.claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md 'Standing orders'
erreicht durch: tests/test_ui_contract.py freezes the shape (named in the protocol and in docs/HANDOFF.md:1115)
läuftINVARIANT dissent is recorded, never averaged
ADR-012 §5 gives the two independent reasons: a majority IS an average and would not even be well defined under degraded quorum ('3 of 4' and '2 of 2' render identically, so availability would decide correctness); and N-model majority voting is LLM-as-judge ensembling, most confident exactly where all participants share a training blind spot — the one case the council exists to catch.
docs/adrs/012 §5; .claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:88 ("Dissent is documented, never averaged away")
erreicht durch: council: no aggregation step, turns stored verbatim per author, round 1 blind and mandatory, max_rounds default 2 hard-capped; crew: an overruled Momus objection goes in the commit message
läuftINVARIANT I1 — raw evidence first; projections are disposable
Source, documents, runtime events, test output and diffs stay authoritative; embeddings, layouts, scores and clusters are deletable versioned projections. ADR-011 §4 sharpens it into a join rule: 'A projection hit is never a join' — a vector result must resolve to a spine intent_id or a ledger id before it can appear in a receipt.
docs/IKARUS_ARIADNE_MASTER_PLAN.md:193 (I1)
erreicht durch: implemented in daedalus/memory/embeddings.py (v1 vectors quarantined rather than attributed to a model); restated by ADR-008, ADR-011 §2, LATENT_PROJECTION_INDEX.md and FOUNDATION_AUDIT
läuftINVARIANT I5 — no silent model mixing
A model change creates a NEW index; searches select exactly one index_id so projections from different coordinate systems are never compared. OLLAMA_EMBED_MODEL_REVISION exists specifically because a local Ollama tag can be moved or overwritten. One of only two invariants with real mechanical enforcement.
docs/IKARUS_ARIADNE_MASTER_PLAN.md:222 (I5)
erreicht durch: daedalus/memory/embeddings.py EmbeddingSpec.index_id binds provider/model/revision/dimension/normalization/projector_version; documented in docs/LATENT_PROJECTION_INDEX.md
läuftINVARIANT the gate decides, not a model
Also stated in the master plan kill list as 'LLM-as-judge als einzige Promotion' being forbidden, and in §12.2 'LLM-as-judge ist Soft Metric mit Modellversion, nicht alleiniger Hard Gate'. The only constitution clause with a test that goes red if it is violated.
docs/adrs/012-council-cross-vendor-review.md §1 ("THE GATE DECIDES, NOT A MODEL"); .claude/skills/council/SKILL.md
erreicht durch: enforced structurally by daedalus/council/ carrying no verdict-shaped field, and by tests/test_council_bus.py:365 + tests/test_council_session.py:114 name-match assertions; GateResult.passed and AttemptResult.state in daedalus/spine/attempt.py are the only verdicts
läuftINVARIANT under-report rather than guess; never silently exclude or truncate
The product-side reason is stated once and is the sharpest sentence in the protocol: 'a fabricated clone cluster — telling a user that unrelated code is duplicated — is worse than a missed one', and 'a duplication report that quietly shrank is indistinguishable from a codebase that got cleaner'. When ambiguous: return <anonymous>, drop the edge, exclude the language.

Korrekturen am Inventar von heute Mittag

  • CHECKED all 12 entries in FEATURE_INVENTORY.json's 'Planned (spec-only)' area, plus its islands/stale lists for doc-adjacent claims. Ten of the twelve are accurate. Two need correction and several need added entrypoints.
  • CHANGED 'Hermes upstream comms service' from status planned -> stale. ADR-002's status is REJECTED after implementation audit, not deferred; calling a rejected decision 'planned' misreads it as backlog. The inventory's note that pycache remnants remain is CONFIRMED — daedalus/hermes/ still exists containing only __pycache__/ and zero .py files.
  • CHANGED 'Mission Spine (MissionSpec, durable state machine)' — still correctly planned as specified (no daedalus/missions/, no MissionSpec, no crash recovery), but the inventory is unaware that daedalus/spine/{ledger,attempt,cancel,picker}.py landed (commit a508da5) under ADR-011, and unaware ADR-011 exists at all. The spine that landed is deliberately NARROWER than the master plan's Mission Spine and its own docstring says so.
  • ADDED (missing entirely from the inventory): docs/adrs/011-event-spine.md, docs/adrs/012-council-cross-vendor-review.md, docs/EXPERIMENT_A_B.md, docs/ENGINE_PARITY.md, docs/PROJECT_SCOPE.md, .claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md, LICENSE, and the absence of a root CLAUDE.md and of an ADR index.
  • ADDED entrypoint to 'The Grove': the inventory cites only master plan §10.3; docs/adrs/009-ariadne-forest-evolution-engine.md also defines it and is the naming authority.
  • ADDED entrypoints to 'Cerberus process-boundary enforcement': the inventory cites only docs/bypasses.md; docs/adrs/007-root-of-trust.md and master plan §16.2 (twelve protection layers) and §16.3 (kill switch) also specify it.
  • FLAGGED as newly false: ADR-012 §7 states session.py ships offline-replay-only with no wiring until the falsification measurement exists. `daedalus council` and `daedalus canary` are live CLI commands (daedalus/cli.py:840-843, commit c348e0e) and no measurement artifact exists. The inventory predates both commits.
  • FLAGGED as still false in code, contrary to any impression the ADRs give: ADR-010's actor-namespace rule. ADR-011 §5 designates daedalus/spine/ledger.py as the enforcement point; grep for 'actor' in that file returns zero matches. ADR-011 predicted this outcome in its own Consequences and the prediction has held.
  • CORRECTED the master plan's own environment claim, per docs/HANDOFF.md 'Environment facts': the plan says the artifact disk is D: everywhere; the actual big disk is E: (2.3 TiB free). The plan has not been updated.
  • NOTED a naming defect ADR-010 should have caught: the crew's test-dev agent is 'Talos' in .claude/agents/test-dev.md:3,6 and 'Týr' in .claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:80. One actor, two names, same repo.
  • NOTED an undocumented port collision: daedalus/web_api.py defaults to 127.0.0.1:8765 and runs/council/room_server.py also defaults to 8765. README's quickstart and HANDOFF's room section both advertise 8765 without mentioning the conflict.

Offen / unklar

  • I did not verify whether the §7 council measurement was run and its output stored outside the repo. I checked runs/council/ (two council transcripts plus room.jsonl from 2026-07-28) and grepped daedalus/council/ and daedalus/eval/ for inter/intra-vendor agreement code and found only the restated protocol in docstrings. To close: read the two runs/council/*.jsonl transcripts and confirm whether either is the control-arm experiment rather than a working review.
  • I did not verify whether the open cleanup_worktree CRITICAL is still open. HANDOFF (written this session) says it is and says the fix is uncommitted; git status shows daedalus/kairos/worktree.py among untracked/modified paths, which is consistent, but I did not run the reproduction and was instructed not to run tests. To close: an owner decision on whether round 2 landed.
  • docs/HANDOFF.md was not in my assigned list but is the de-facto entry point and supersedes several docs that WERE in my list. I read its top 215 lines and §2 only. The 900 lines of historical sessions below the fold contain test counts and architecture claims that its own header disowns; I have not audited them for claims that might still be cited.
  • I cannot tell whether ADR-001, 003, 004, 005, 006, 007 have been re-read since the ADR-011/012 work landed. Their Proposed statuses are consistent with today's code, but nothing records a review date, so 'still accurate' and 'not looked at' are indistinguishable. A dated 'last verified against code' line per ADR would fix this and does not exist.
  • The 'archive is permanent on every resource' invariant is my reconstruction from four separate statements (ADR-006, ADR-009, ADR-011 §3, master plan §10.3). There is no single document that states it as one invariant, and the guarantee strength genuinely differs per store. If the owner meant something narrower by 'every resource' — e.g. an ArtifactStore pin/release contract — that specific formulation exists only in master plan §10.3 and is unimplemented.
  • BYOK has no ADR. It is a standing order in .claude/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md:163 and a Cerberus review criterion, both crew-side artifacts, plus one product docstring in daedalus/ikarus_os.py:10. For a constitution-level invariant that constrains the business model, that is the thinnest documentary footing of the nine.
  • I did not audit docs/COMMS_PROTOCOL.md, docs/MISSION_CONTROL.md or docs/FALLBACK.md against current code. All three are pre-audit (2026-07-05/11) and all three are still linked from README as current, so any drift in them is presented to readers as fact. This is the highest-value remaining doc audit.
  • structcore-rs parity is documented twice (bypasses.md §5, ENGINE_PARITY.md) and both records self-limit: ENGINE_PARITY explicitly could not build or run Rust because cargo was not on PATH. Whether the Rust engine is currently selectable by packaging — the specific risk bypasses.md names — I did not check.

memory and provenance — daedalus/memory/ (journal + embeddings), daedalus/memstore.py, daedalus/dctx.py, daedalus/storage.py, daedalus/council/ (bus, vendors, session, publish, canary) 87 1 kaputt 2 nur Spec 17 Insel 3 dunkel 3 Shim 61 läuft

There are now SIX durable stores in this area, not four, and ADR-011 (docs/adrs/011-event-spine.md, status "Proposed") only names four. (1) The operational JSONL journal at memory/events.local.jsonl (daedalus/memory/__init__.py) — plain open("a"), no lock, no chain, records carry NO id; it is written today by file_bridge.py:174 and token_monitor.py and read by status.py:48 and GET /api/events/memory. ADR-011 §2 demotes it to "independent operational domain, not load-bearing; no receipt may cite it". (2) The dmem/1 hash-chained ledger (daedalus/memstore.py, memory/ledger.local.jsonl) — the only tamper-evident store in the area, fully built and heavily tested, and a complete ISLAND: zero non-test importers, and neither memory/ledger.local.jsonl nor memory/state.local.json exists on disk. (3) The SQLite vector projection index (daedalus/memory/embeddings.py, memory/vectors.db) — schema v2, derived and discardable, self-declared non-citable; memory/vectors.db does not exist on this machine, so both of its wired readers currently return nothing. (4) The SQLite WAL intent ledger daedalus/spine/ledger.py, which ADR-011 §1 elects as THE SPINE and ordering authority — it exists and spine/attempt.py uses it, but ADR-011's three preconditions are UNIMPLEMENTED: grep finds no `actor` field, no `^(daedalus|crew)\.` refusal, no attestation helper, and nothing anywhere writes an intent_id into the memory ledger or the journal. ADR-011 §3's "asymmetric and mandatory" attestation duty is therefore satisfied ZERO times, which by the ADR's own consequences section means no spine transition currently has proof. (5) The dcouncil/1 council bus (daedalus/council/bus.py, runs/council/<id>.jsonl) — a fifth chained store, landed after ADR-011 and not covered by it; ADR-011 §5 forbids "a fifth authoritative log", and bus.py resolves the tension by refusing loudly to write anywhere under <repo>/memory/ and by stating COUNCIL RECORDS ARE NEVER AN INPUT TO MEMORY RECALL (because memstore.fold_state promotes at three confirmations and four models agreeing would be three confirmations). (6) runs/canary/history.jsonl — an unchained append-only JSONL of vendor health, also uncovered by ADR-011. The stores share no join key and no sequence: the journal cannot be pointed at (embeddings must synthesize an id as f"{time}_{kind}", memory/__init__.py:86), and the only two stores that CAN be joined (spine + memstore) are not joined by any code. Council is the most-recently-landed and the most-exercised: `daedalus council` and `daedalus canary` are both wired in cli.py, and I verified three real transcripts on disk chain-verify clean (verify_chain ok=True on both council-*.jsonl and room.jsonl) plus 20 real canary probe records across 4 lanes. The advisory doctrine is enforced structurally, not by docstring: CouncilRecord/VendorReply carry no approve/reject/score/majority field and tests/test_council_session.py:132 walks every field of every nested dataclass to keep it that way. The independence rule is independence_class = (vendor, model_family) with model_family deliberately over-merging (strips :tag and trailing size/version tokens), so same-vendor seats whose model ids resolve to one family are ONE class — and when a CLI cannot report its model the family is the literal "unknown" (both real transcripts show council.anthropic.unknown), so three unnamed Anthropic seats collapse to one class; convene() catches that case via actor-id collision (session.py:972) rather than via the class check, and the ANOMALY reason "unnamed_model" that exists for it is dead vocabulary no code path emits.

InselTwo-SHA discipline (body_sha content fingerprint + entry_sha chain link)
body_sha excludes ts/prev/entry_sha/id so identical bodies dedupe; entry_sha = sha256(prev+\0+body_sha+\0+ts) binds order. This exclusion is exactly why ADR-011 §1 disqualifies it as a sequencer: two distinct intents with identical bodies collapse to one record.
daedalus/memstore.py:130 _body_sha / :137 _entry_sha
erreicht durch: memstore internals only (no production caller)

läuftcanary liveness-vs-quality split and the exit code
arrival/instruction are LIVENESS (transport alarm, trips the exit code); anchoring/comprehension are QUALITY (reported loudly, does NOT trip it). Merging them would either bury the transport alarm or page a human because a 7b model failed a reading task. Written after a real 2026-07-28 incident: codex received a prompt truncated at the first newline by an npm .cmd shim, and a local model answered by anchoring on its own prior turn — both are GREEN to an exit-0/HTTP-200 liveness check.
daedalus/council/canary.py:658 ('the split that must never collapse'), CanaryRun.exit_code (:694), summary (:659)
erreicht durch: cli.py:762 returns run.exit_code()

läuftCouncil profiles are completions, not agents (no write path by construction)
Deliberately NOT adapters.subprocess_adapter.RUNTIME_PROFILES (those carry --sandbox workspace-write and --permission-mode dontAsk in the primary checkout). claude: -p --output-format json --disallowed-tools=<15 tools> --strict-mcp-config, no permission-mode at all. codex: --ask-for-approval never exec --sandbox read-only --skip-git-repo-check with the prompt on stdin. Reason is evidence provenance as much as containment: an agentic reviewer fetches its own bytes, so two vendors citing 'the file' may have read different bytes (docs/bypasses.md §2).

läuftCouncil secret floor — per path, per text, never over an assembled blob
Inbound scanning is the load-bearing part: once a secret is inside a hash-chained line it can never be redacted without breaking verify_chain, so a tamper-evident transcript would become a permanent un-deletable copy of the leak. A hit refuses the turn (no redact-and-send, no redact-and-store) and chains a status="refused" receipt naming only the rule label; the receipt is re-scanned and degrades to anonymous if the identity fields themselves carry the secret.
daedalus/council/bus.py:252 _scan_paths, :262 _scan_texts, :280 _scan_turn, :299 _scan_roster; refusal receipt at :445 _refusal_receipt
erreicht durch: every append_round/append_turn/append_roster, on OUTBOUND turns and on INBOUND vendor responses BEFORE they are chained
läuftCouncil store may never be written under <repo>/memory/
The structural expression of 'COUNCIL RECORDS ARE NEVER AN INPUT TO MEMORY RECALL'. Rationale in the docstring: memstore.fold_state promotes at three confirmations, and four models agreeing about a patch would be three confirmations — promoting model opinion to certified memory is named as the worst failure in this project. tests/test_council_vendors.py:672 additionally asserts vendors.py never imports or writes memstore.

erreicht durch: every _CliAdapter dispatch
läuftdctx anti-tautology rule (label_provenance travels with recall forever)
The rule: labels come from the CALLER or not at all; no labels → recall is None, NEVER 1.0 ('a vacuous 1.0 is the lie'). Refuses labels without provenance and provenance without labels. hand_reachable is accepted but recorded as the circular case so a consumer can segregate it — the docstring names daedalus/eval/tasks.py and eval/harness.py:_recall (returns 1.0 for an empty label list) as the live circularity this exists to refuse.
daedalus/dctx.py:61 LABEL_PROVENANCE = (hand_reachable, independent_diff, temporal_churn, none); validation at :180-188; verify-side invariants at :324 _recall_claim_failures
erreicht durch: compile() and verify()
läuftdctx compile() — certified context receipt (dctx/1)
Thin wrapper over structcore.slice.semantic_slice — adds no context logic. receipt_sha covers STRUCTURAL claims only (_canonical at :112): token counts, backend, absolute paths and timestamps are deliberately excluded because they move with the install, not the code. Stated caveat: with max_tokens set, the kept set depends on the tokenizer, so only un-budgeted receipts are tokenizer-independent.
daedalus/dctx.py:158; CLI at daedalus/cli.py:812-813 `daedalus dctx <repo> <target>`
erreicht durch: `daedalus dctx` only. NOTHING in offload.py, structcore/slice.py, spine/ or council/ mints a receipt.
läuftdctx egress verdict — independent second opinion over the included set
Records only the rule NAME, never matched text ('a certificate that quoted the secret it certifies the absence of would be an own-goal'). Catches a secret planted into an already-included file AFTER minting.

läuftdctx unit digest bound to identity (path + role + mode + file bytes)

läuftdctx verify() — offline, no model, no network
Re-derives commit, per-unit digests and the egress predicate from disk, plus the receipt's own sha. Explicitly does NOT reproduce the recall number (that would be a recompile) — it checks internal consistency and the anti-tautology invariants. Returns specific failures, never a bare False.

läuftEmbeddingSpec identity — the six fields that determine index_id
index_id = "emb:" + sha256(canonical_json({provider, model, model_revision, dimension, normalization, projector_version})). ALL SIX fields are in the hash — change any one and you get a different, non-comparable index. Frozen dataclass with __post_init__ validation (non-empty model, positive dimension, normalization in {l2,none}). Queries compare vectors from exactly one index_id (search_report:983).

läuftHard bounds, all fail-closed and all recorded (token ceiling charged BEFORE dispatch)
A per-call timeout does not bound token spend because transcript tokens grow O(V^2 R^2), hence the pre-dispatch charge. A hung vendor is abandoned on a daemon thread and recorded unavailable/timeout.

läuftIndependence class = (vendor, model_family); three same-family heads are ONE class
model_family strips the :tag and trailing size/version tokens and deliberately OVER-merges ('over-merging costs a seat; under-merging silently fakes independence'), so local qwen2.5-coder:7b and bench qwen2.5-coder:7b collide as one voice on two sockets, and same-vendor seats resolving to one family are one class. When a CLI cannot report its model the family is the literal 'unknown', so three unnamed Anthropic seats are one class. Korrekturen am Inventar von heute Mittag

  • CHECKED all 4 entries the prior docs/FEATURE_INVENTORY.json lists under 'Memory (journal, embeddings, certified ledger)'. Verdicts below.
  • CONFIRMED unchanged: 'Vector index auto-embedding on append' = dark. The gate is DAEDALUS_VECTOR_INDEX at daedalus/memory/__init__.py:65, default unset, set nowhere in the repo; the synchronous-embed P0 is real and still open (docs/HANDOFF.md:355, docs/LATENT_PROJECTION_INDEX.md:73-85).
  • CONFIRMED and strengthened: 'memstore certified memory ledger (dmem/1)' = island. Zero non-test importers anywhere under daedalus/ (grep for `from .memstore`/`import memstore` returns only tests, two of which import it purely to assert the council never touches it), AND memory/ledger.local.jsonl / memory/state.local.json do not exist on disk. It has never been written.
  • CORRECTED: 'Event journal + latent search API' was listed as wired with 'explicit ready/partial/embedder_unavailable states'. The journal half is wired, but the endpoint credited with those states cannot report them — daedalus/web_api.py:435 calls the compatibility wrapper store.search(), which returns .matches and drops the OperationStatus, so embedder_unavailable, index_unavailable and invalid_index are indistinguishable from 'no results'. Separately, memory/vectors.db does not exist, so the endpoint returns [] today and creates an empty database as a side effect of being called.
  • CORRECTED/REFINED: 'Latent Projection Index v2' was listed as wired. The store class is wired, but its only non-dark production reader is context_plan.latent_memory_seed_scores, itself behind a default-off --latent / ?latent=1 flag; the DAEDALUS_VECTOR_INDEX writer is dark; and index_status(), legacy_unversioned_count(), ingest_transport_records*(), ingest_events() and _embed_batch() have no production caller at all. The quarantined-v1 count is therefore never surfaced to any operator.
  • ADDED (entirely missing from the prior inventory): daedalus/dctx.py — certified context receipts, the anti-tautology label_provenance rule, offline verify(), the egress-policy fingerprint, and the finding that the shipped `daedalus dctx` CLI exposes no --label flag so every CLI-minted receipt is label_provenance='none' / recall=null.
  • ADDED (missing): daedalus/storage.py — the fail-closed storage watermark, wired from kairos/worktree.py:583 and spine/attempt.py:713/770.
  • ADDED (missing entirely): all five daedalus/council/ modules (bus, vendors, session, publish, canary), the `daedalus council` and `daedalus canary` CLI commands, ADR-012, and the evidence that both have actually run (3 chain-verified transcripts, 20 canary records).
  • ADDED (missing): the Der Raum bus mirror at runs/council/room.py:658 — the council bus's second production writer.
  • ADDED (missing): the ADR-011 event-spine decision and the fact that its §3 attestation duty and §4 cross-store join are UNBUILT (no `actor` field, no namespace refusal, no attestation helper in daedalus/spine/ledger.py; no intent_id written into any other store).
  • NEW FACT, contra ADR-011's 'four durable stores' framing: there are six. The council bus (runs/council/*.jsonl, chained) and the canary history (runs/canary/history.jsonl, unchained) both landed after the ADR and are not covered by it, while ADR-011 §5 explicitly forbids 'a fifth authoritative log'. bus.py resolves the tension by construction — it refuses any store path under <repo>/memory/ and states council records are never an input to memory recall — but the ADR itself has not been updated to say so.
  • NEW DEFECT: the council bus has two writers producing INCOMPATIBLE actor ids for the same weights. session/vendors emit `council.local.qwen2.5-coder-7b` (vendor key 'local', colon slugged) while runs/council/room.py:648 emits `council.ollama.qwen2.5-coder:7b` (vendor from the display name, colon kept — bus._ACTOR_RE permits ':' in the model segment). Both are on disk now. Any per-actor aggregation or cross-store join over the bus counts them as two participants.
  • NEW DEFECT (minor): the ANOMALY reason 'unnamed_model' is declared in both bus.py:119 and session.py:218 and emitted by nothing. Both real transcripts chain `council.anthropic.unknown` as an ordinary status=spoke turn, so an unresolvable model id — which collapses the independence class to (vendor,'unknown') — raises no anomaly.
  • CORRECTION to ADR-012 §2 as implemented: the ADR says the roster 'refuses to seat two participants of the same class, or seats them with duplicate_class=True'. The code only ever does the latter (session.py:1002-1004); the hard refusal (session.py:972) is on duplicate ACTOR ID, not duplicate class.

Offen / unklar

  • UNKNOWN whether the council's own pre-registered falsification has been run. The protocol is stated identically in council/__init__.py, session.py and vendors.py (Arm A = four vendors, Control Arm B = one vendor asked twice under two role prompts, ground truth = spine.attempt.GateResult.passed / AttemptResult.state, falsifier = inter-vendor agreement within noise of intra-vendor agreement ⇒ DELETE THE MODULE). I found no results artifact: runs/council/ holds only transcripts, and docs/EXPERIMENT_A_B.md is a DIFFERENT, explicitly 'designed, not run' experiment (harness vs crew). To resolve I would need a search of runs/ and docs/ for an arm-A/arm-B council result file, or the owner's word. Note the modules say 'NO LIVE WIRING into a real review workflow happens before the number exists' — yet `daedalus council` is already a shipped CLI command, so whether the CLI counts as 'wired into a review workflow' is an owner judgement I cannot make.
  • UNKNOWN whether `daedalus council` has ever completed with four independent classes seated. Both real transcripts show only 2 of 4 answering (google not_authenticated, openai not_on_path in one; a single local seat in the other), and canary history shows google unavailable in all 5 records. Whether that is a local environment state or a defect in AntigravityAdapter's ssh path cannot be determined read-only — it needs a live `daedalus canary --vendors google` run.
  • UNKNOWN whether the anthropic seat's model id can ever be resolved. Both transcripts carry `council.anthropic.unknown` (COUNCIL_PROFILES['anthropic'].default_model = 'unknown', vendors.py:501) — I could not determine read-only whether ClaudeAdapter._parse_ok (vendors.py:801) extracts a model from the CLI's JSON in practice or whether 'unknown' is the permanent outcome. Resolving it needs one live `daedalus council --dry-run` plus a real call, or a read of a captured claude -p --output-format json payload.
  • UNKNOWN: I did not read daedalus/council/bus.py lines ~360-760 or canary.py lines ~490-1190 line-by-line — I outlined their symbols and read the load-bearing sections (_normalize_turn, _refusal_receipt, _chain_records, append_*, verify_chain; probe builders, grade, run_canary, history). Entries for those files are based on symbol outlines plus targeted reads, so a subtle defect inside an unread body would not have been caught.
  • NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION: I did not run the test suite (instructed not to). Every 'tested' claim is from the presence and content of the test file, not from a passing run. The one thing I did execute is bus.verify_chain over the three on-disk transcripts (read-only, no writes).
  • OPEN QUESTION for the owner, not a gap in my reading: nothing consumes CouncilRecord.checkable_claims. The council produces a queue of deterministic checks and no code feeds that queue into the gate, so the module's stated product currently terminates at a human reading render() output.
  • OPEN QUESTION: because memstore has no writer and no reader, its promotion rule (3 confirmations → primary) has never been exercised against real data, and the ADR-011 role assigned to it (attestation sidecar holding landed_edit/gate_outcome/receipt_ref) has no implementation to point at. Anyone reading the docs would reasonably believe certified memory is operating; it is not.

daedalus/structcore/ (Universal Structural Core) + daedalus/eval/ (decontaminated oracle) 119 1 kaputt 1 nur Spec 6 Insel 10 dunkel 2 stale 99 läuft

structcore is 19 modules / ~5,900 lines and is genuinely load-bearing: build_index -> cached_index feeds the web API (/api/structure, /api/topology, /api/context/plan, /api/distill), the CLI (`daedalus context`, `python -m daedalus.structcore`, `python -m daedalus.structcore.slice`), offload, ikarus_os, dctx, provider_router's safety fence, spine/picker, and the whole eval package. PIPELINE: _collect walks the repo (os.walk, hard _IGNORE_DIRS blocklist + every dot-dir skipped) and keeps only files whose extension matches a LanguageSpec (21 specs, 33 extensions -- NO .md/.html/.json/.yaml). project_scope() then splits the collected set into core / shell / outside; shell files are still parsed (so import edges into them stay true) but withheld from every metric. A content-keyed sqlite cache absorbs unchanged files and the remainder runs through a ProcessPoolExecutor (>=100 cache-missing files). Each file yields units + metrics + window-run hashes + raw imports. Import RESOLUTION stays serial in the parent (it needs the whole file set): Python is precise and center-relative-aware via _PyNaming (with alias canonicalisation and refusal-on-ambiguity), everything else is best-effort via imports.resolve_internal. Four clone passes share one CloneMemo: exact/Type-1 (unit_clusters, SHA1 of comment+docstring-stripped source), renamed/Type-2 (abstract_normalize: ident->ID, num->NUM, str->STR; pure Type-1 clusters subtracted out), near-miss/Type-3 (SourcererCC multiset overlap >= 0.8 on abstracted bags, union-find, bounded by inverted-index candidate generation, min_shared_rare=4, max_cluster=30, C/C++ EXCLUDED and the exclusion reported), and window (universal, parser-free, 6-line normalized runs grouped by the file-set that shares them). Every cluster is safety-annotated: any site under a devices/hv/motion/interlock-shaped path is stamped "judgment-gated -- do NOT auto-collapse". semantic_slice picks context from import_edges (rel->rel, all languages) + import_edges_reverse, and its safety gate is three-layered: (1) a FOCUS GATE that scans the FULL focus text and fails CLOSED, (2) a neighbour egress gate applied at each EMISSION point, not just to the rel sets, (3) a shell-boundary re-assertion (`u.module in modules`) on the symbol path so the resolver cannot smuggle a vendored body in. Withholding is never silent -- sorted `withheld` block + inline breadcrumb + shell_boundary_stops. Forest+DSS sit on top: build_knowledge_forest turns the index into a multiplex graph (nodes=source files; layers=imports/co_change; clone clusters kept as HYPEREDGES, never expanded to fake cliques) and semantic_super_sample runs restriction (sum relevance to ancestors) -> bounded prolongation (top-4 children per branch, pruned mass NOT renormalised) -> per-relation diffusion -> weighted fusion -> greedy token-budget packing -> a content-addressed DSSReceipt. eval/ is the decontaminated oracle. Provenance is never blended: hand_reachable (10 hardcoded tasks in tasks.py) is printed PARTIALLY SELF-GRADED because a human picked the label AND verified it by running the very slicer being graded; independent_diff labels come from a byte diff of what a commit literally changed, with an explicit no-graph-walk rule, cross-file-only, plus junk/cross-language/secret-floor hygiene filters. All 17 minted tasks in minted_tasks.json are tier=quarantine, confirmations=0 -- so they are in every render and in NO go/no-go number. --gate is an advisory per-task recall ratchet against baseline.json (never a mean, never wired to block anything); ceiling.py measures the temporal-enrichment upper bound with a backtest-clean arm (git log <sha>^) and a leaky arm, and prints a machine reopen signal against a materiality floor. CRITICAL FINDING: I believe with HIGH confidence that PnP_App returns 0 files because pnp_app.json declares center = ["app","src","design/visual-lab/src"] while `app/` and `src/` do not exist on that disk (the config's own _center_comment says so), so every collected file is classified shell and n_files = len(records) - len(ignored) = 0; the 137 markdown files are invisible for a second, independent reason (there is no .md LanguageSpec, so spec_for returns None and they are never collected at all).

läuftmint label hygiene: secret-floor anchor + label exclusion
A floor-tripping file is dropped from BOTH the anchor pool (semantic_slice fails it closed as a focus) and label consideration (the same gate withholds it as a neighbour in every lane), because such a label would be a guaranteed permanent miss mislabeled as a slicer defect. If every anchor trips, nothing mints -- it never falls back to an unsafe anchor.

The regex fallback is in imports.py:95 (_REGEX_FALLBACK, 11 languages) and covers IMPORT extraction only, never unit extraction.

Offen / unklar

  • PnP_App 0-files HYPOTHESIS (high confidence on the mechanism; no scan run). Two INDEPENDENT causes, and they must not be conflated. (1) The 137 markdown files can never be seen by any scan: languages.py has no LanguageSpec for .md (nor .html/.json/.yaml/.toml), so index._collect's `spec_for(fn) is None -> continue` (index.py:53-55) drops them before scope is even consulted. This is certain, not a hypothesis. (2) The 25 source files: projects/pnp_app.json declares center = ['app','src','design/visual-lab/src']. I listed the repo root — it contains only .claude/, .git/, claude_ttrpg_prompt_pack/, design/, CLAUDE.md, STATUS.md. There is NO app/ and NO src/. ProjectScope.in_center (ignore.py:199) is a pure string prefix test with zero filesystem validation, so every collected file is is_shell() -> lands in `ignored` -> n_files = len(records) - len(ignored) = 0 (index.py:539). RESIDUAL I could not resolve without running a scan: design/visual-lab/src DOES exist and holds exactly 3 spec-matching files (App.tsx, main.tsx, styles.css), so with TODAY's config a fresh build should report n_files=3, not 0. Most likely the measurement predates that third center entry (pnp_app.json mtime 13:17 today; HANDOFF.md 15:40 today) or was run with center=['app','src'] only. TO CONFIRM in one read without guessing: print idx['ignored'] — count==25 and n_files_scanned==25 proves scope classification; count==0 with n_files==0 would instead prove a collection failure. Also worth ruling out: DAEDALUS_CENTER set in the measuring shell (ignore.py:246), which would produce the identical all-shell result on ANY unconfigured repo.
  • The eval oracle indexes UNSCOPED while the product indexes SCOPED. harness.py:206+279, harness.py:491+572, mint.py:327 and ceiling.py:249 all call cached_index(repo) with NO center/ignore, whereas web_api._structure_index passes projects/agent_env.json's center=['daedalus','apps/web/src'] and ignore=['@tests','daedalus/eval/fixtures/']. So the eval's `modules` set (which IS the slice scope boundary and mint's in-scope test) includes tests/, docs/, runs/ and every other tree the shipping product deliberately withholds, and the compression denominator is a different repo from the one /api/distill measures. Not obviously wrong — dogfooding the whole repo is defensible — but it is undeclared, and any comparison between an eval compression figure and a product one is comparing two different denominators.
  • languages.py declares safety_content regexes per language (Rust unsafe, C volatile/__asm, Java Runtime.exec, JS eval(), C# DllImport) and the module docstring says 'the safety fence uses safety_content regexes to raise the risk tier'. I found no code that reads spec.safety_content — clones._safety/_is_safety_path is purely PATH-based. Either the content tier was never built or it lives somewhere I did not grep; status recorded as unknown rather than guessed.
  • daedalus/eval/minted_tasks.json hardcodes repo='C:/Users/nukei/Desktop/agent_env' (absolute, machine-specific) on all 17 tasks. resolve_task_repo accepts an absolute path only if Path(repo).exists(), so on any other machine or after a move all 17 become ERRORED rows. They are quarantine-tier so the gate would not fail, but the independent corpus would silently evaporate from every render.
  • The advisory gate ratchets only the self-graded corpus. baseline.json holds 10 entries — exactly the hand_reachable primary tasks. Every independent_diff task is quarantine, and run_gate skips non-primary tiers (harness.py:865). So the one number the ratchet protects is precisely the one report.py labels PARTIALLY SELF-GRADED, and no independent label can regress the gate until three confirmations promote it.
  • Four confirmed islands in structcore: graph.name_index (graph.py:49, zero callers anywhere including tests), churn.temporal_misses (churn.py:215, tests only), clones.window_clusters (clones.py:451, tests only), index._hotspots (index.py:682, zero callers). Also parse.python_imports/python_import_records (tests only). None is harmful; all five would fail a 'someone calls this' check.
  • DSS temporal carry is live code with permanently empty input: carry_temporal_scores/rename_map/rename_confidence are plumbed all the way through context_plan.plan_context, but neither cli._context nor the /api/context/plan handler ever supplies them. Likewise DSSConfig.unknown_relation_weight=0.0 means any NEW forest relation layer contributes exactly nothing until someone adds a weight — a silent no-op rather than an error.
  • metrics.py:96 computes a local `ext` that is never used (dead line inside _lizard_cc).
  • I did not open tests/ in depth — I verified test FILES exist for every claim (31 relevant files) and grepped for specific callers, but I did not read the assertions, so 'tested' in my notes means 'a test file names this symbol', not 'the behaviour is proven'. I also never ran a test or a scan, per the read-only brief, so every 'measured' figure I quote (2.3% ceiling, 7.68x TCT scoping, 141 .py/.pyi collisions) is quoted FROM the source comments and docs, not reproduced by me.

The session record — docs/HANDOFF.md (1136 lines, newest-first), docs/adrs/, the docs/ corpus, and C:/Users/nukei/.claude/projects/c--Users-nukei-Desktop-agent-env/memory/*.md — read in full, and the PROJECT NARRATIVE of decisions and reversals reconstructed from them, with every load-bearing claim re-checked against the working tree. 66 3 kaputt 4 nur Spec 2 Insel 2 dunkel 6 stale 1 Baseline 2 unklar 46 läuft

The record reads as one continuous arc: a project_tct-specific delegation harness (Jul 5-6) becomes "Daedalus, the Agent OS" (Jul 19), then "an evolutionary engine for code" (Jul 20), then "Ikarus + Ariadne" with a self-improvement spine and a cross-vendor council (Jul 28). What actually drives it is not the roadmap — it is a repeating cycle in which a confident claim is measured, found wrong, and withdrawn in writing. I count at least fifteen documented reversals: the Rust engine's "10-100x" (measured ~1.3x, SLOWER on the full repo), the eval's "100% recall" (partly tautological, now literally printed PARTIALLY SELF-GRADED at daedalus/eval/report.py:18), the temporal co-change tier (claimed 19% of misses, measured 1/43 = 2.3% rename-aware, closed and replaced by a reopen gate at daedalus/eval/ceiling.py with REOPEN_MIN_SHARE=0.10), the Ollama "HALVING LAW" (a truncation penalty, not a window; corrected to full num_ctx minus OUTPUT_RESERVE_TOKENS=1024), "structurally impossible" candidate containment (refuted by Codex — an in-process runner callable is not a jail), and the security fix that shipped with 29 green tests and was then shown, through the public API, to still delete the repository. Three method rules were paid for in blood and are now doctrine: a number measured under load is WRONG not slow (1.47x was really 0.99x; 499 fabricated clones were really 66); a green suite is not evidence (three guards survive their own deletion); and a gate agent's verdict must be re-verified in a fresh process (Cerberus found a "cleared" egress gate still leaking through six bypass classes). The record is honest about itself — it stamps [M]/[I]/[A], it refuses to retro-edit old numbers, and it names its own agents' mistakes. Two things are OPEN and blocking as of the top of HANDOFF.md: the repo-deletion CRITICAL (round 2 in progress; daedalus/spine/attempt.py is still untracked and daedalus/kairos/worktree.py still modified) and the generalisation failure on the sibling repo PnP_App. Against the working tree I found the record has drifted in five places — three of its open items are already closed in code (the auto-mint flywheel seam, the backslash latent-search route, the RTX env-var drift), one of its claims is simply false today (`import daedalus.metron` raises ModuleNotFoundError), and the PnP_App "sees nothing" framing is partly explained by that project's own declared center.

läuftThe doctrine — 7 steps, and dogfooding as method
verify-before-build -> disjoint-ownership lanes -> mechanical gates -> adversarial refutation (3 skeptics defaulting to refuted, >=2 failures-to-refute triggers repair) -> record with provenance in the same beat -> LET THE MEASUREMENT PICK THE NEXT MOVE -> feed the flywheel. Step 6 is the one with a live example in the record: 61.7% recall -> triage showed 0% structural drops -> temporal enrichment jumped the queue ahead of the offload wire, and was then itself refuted.
memory/daedalus-modus-operandi.md
erreicht durch: Cited and visibly followed in every session record from 2026-07-21 on
läuftThe eval gate is ADVISORY and must stay that way
Three preconditions are written down before it may ever block: an independent label source, a live round showing gate decisions correlate with task success/rollback rates, and explicit risk sign-off. The standing invariant across all memory files is 'never let an unvalidated metric gate autonomy — advisory first.'

room.md is a real cross-vendor adversarial review, and it demonstrably produced things single-vendor review did not. Claude opened with a load-bearing claim ("TaskAttempt makes it structurally impossible for a candidate model to write the primary checkout — by construction"). Codex REFUTED it with 6 findings, 3 CRITICAL, all with file:line. Claude verified one independently before acting, conceded the framing on two more, and disputed one. Opus 4.6 then ruled on the dispute and added two findings BOTH other vendors had missed. A fresh Claude (Fable) with no session context attacked the process itself: that Claude's retreat position ("no promotion path applies a patch") was never verified by anyone, that Opus adjudicated an Anthropic-vs-OpenAI dispute in Anthropic's favour on docstring evidence, and that the fix for a refuted design was being written and trusted by the same instance that wrote the bug, with the reviewer stood down. The local Ollama contributed nothing as a reviewer (five near-identical "I could not break it") but its failure mode became the most reusable artifact of the day: daedalus/council/canary.py, a deterministic four-probe vendor health check whose docstring names the two silent-breakage incidents (codex prompt truncated by an npm .cmd shim; Ollama anchoring on its own prior turn) that a "process exited 0 / HTTP 200" liveness check would report green through. VERIFIED AGAINST CODE: the junction→rmtree CRITICAL is genuinely fixed and heavily tested (worktree.py fully rewritten, 39 tests); the directional-containment blind spot Opus found is fixed and tested; the worktree-root containment HIGH is fixed before mkdir. NOT fixed: the verb-only git allowlist still permits `config`, `diff`, `cat-file` against the primary checkout with no argument-shape or env validation, while attempt.py's docstring still asserts "none of them writes" — Codex showed `--ext-diff`/`--textconv` execute arbitrary helpers; the two-intent ledger fix everyone agreed on is not implemented (cleanup still runs in `finally:` BEFORE terminal resolution); the branch-reap fix Opus's finding produced is an island with no production caller; and OS-level sandboxing (the category fix for Codex's two other CRITICALs) is spec-only. Fable's sharpest point is still true: nothing adversarially reviewed the patch. On the operational side the transcript and the code around it record five distinct multi-vendor failure modes with fixes at named lines — of which two are only half-landed: model-keyed cursors are WRITTEN but never READ, and vendor errors are still appended as speech by the GUI path (three live instances in room.md, one leaking an OAuth redirect with scopes and state). FEATURE_INVENTORY.json is not merely stale documentation — daedalus/spine/picker.py:89 consumes it as the top-priority ranked work source for `daedalus improve`, and it has no generator, no freshness gate, and no test. It contains zero entries for daedalus/spine/ (still filed as "planned"), zero for daedalus/council/, zero for runs/council/, and its one worktree entry restates precisely the isolation claim Codex refuted.

kaputtFEATURE_INVENTORY error — "Git worktree isolation | wired | isolates candidate writes from the primary checkout"
The parenthetical "(not a host sandbox)" is the only hedge, and it is doing far more work than it can carry. The module does not isolate candidate WRITES — it makes the manager's own cleanup fail-closed. A candidate's in-process Python still writes wherever the process can. Suggested correction: status stays `wired` for the mechanism, but the note must say it contains the MANAGER's destructive operations, not the candidate's writes, and must name the missing OS sandbox.

kaputtLESSON — cursor keying: a cursor belongs to (speaker, model), not to the speaker; a slot is a ROLE and different weights can occupy it
THE DOCUMENTED FIX DOES NOT TAKE EFFECT. The incident is recorded precisely at room.py:259-265: "the ollama slot ran qwen2.5-coder, advancing its cursor to 34; devstral then took the same slot, inherited that cursor, and was sent ZERO turns while the caller had explicitly asked for the transcript. It answered generically, from nothing, and nothing in the output said so." Both devstral turns are in the transcript (room.md:418, :424) and both are in the chained mirror as council.ollama.devstral:latest. Live state confirms the asymmetry: runs/council/cursors.json holds {"ollama": 36} — unkeyed — against 46 turns in room.md; room_server.py:143 also calls room.say(who, text) with no model at all. Net effect: model-keyed cursors are written by one path, read by none, so a lane either re-reads everything (O(N^2) tokens, the cost the cursor exists to avoid) or inherits another model's cursor exactly as before.

läuft`council` skill (.claude/skills/council/SKILL.md)
Encodes the doctrine that keeps this area honest: advisory only, no majority/consensus/score field and "the absence of the field IS the control", dissent rendered verbatim ahead of convergence, "unanimity is not confirmation" (four models on overlapping data agreeing is weak evidence), "the premise is untested", and the degraded-quorum rule that a short roster is the FIRST thing reported, not a footnote. The two live council runs were degraded and this rule is why that is visible.
.claude/skills/council/SKILL.md (project-scoped)
erreicht durch: The Skill tool; points at daedalus/council/session.py and daedalus/council/publish.py.
läuftCouncil session / convene (daedalus/council/session.py)
CouncilRecord carries NO approve/reject/score/majority/consensus/confidence field and a test walks every field of every nested dataclass to keep it absent — "ABSENCE OF THE FIELD is the control" (:9-16), because a majority field is a promotion token one line away from gating a merge. Round 1 is blind and mandatory so nobody anchors on a first speaker and no injected instruction propagates as an apparently-authored turn (:26-30). Both live runs recorded degraded=true with empty content from the google and openai lanes.

läuftCouncil transcript bus (daedalus/council/bus.py, dcouncil/1)
The most important design decision in the council area, stated at bus.py:13-21: it deliberately REIMPLEMENTS memstore.py's discipline rather than importing it, and refuses any store path under <repo>/memory/, because memstore.fold_state promotes an entry to `primary` after three independent confirmations — and four models generating confident prose about a codebase would be three confirmations. "COUNCIL RECORDS ARE NEVER AN INPUT TO MEMORY RECALL." Tail truncation is caught only against the persisted head/count anchor, which is why room.jsonl.anchor.json exists.

läuftDer Raum engine (runs/council/room.py)
Three things are deliberately borrowed from daedalus rather than reinvented (room.py:17-29): attachments are DISTILLED via structcore.semantic_slice + parse.extract_units, every turn is mirrored into the hash-chained daedalus.council.bus, and a per-speaker unread cursor replaces resending the room. Governing rule: "Nothing here is silent. A withheld file, a trimmed slice, an omitted turn and a held floor are all NAMED in the prompt the speaker actually receives." Tested by tests/test_room_wiring.py (40 tests).

läuftdocs/FEATURE_INVENTORY.json — as a live input to autonomous work
THE MOST IMPORTANT THING ABOUT THIS FILE AND IT IS RECORDED NOWHERE IN IT: the inventory is not documentation, it is the top-priority ranked work queue for the self-improvement loop. Islands outrank stale artifacts, both outrank eval misses, all outrank hotspots (picker.py:25-30, stated as a human-chosen PRIOR so a reviewer can argue with it). A stale inventory therefore does not merely mislead a reader — it aims autonomous attempts at the wrong targets and hides the newest subsystems from the loop entirely. Mitigation in the code: load_inventory degrades to {} rather than raising (:286-292), and only STRUCTURED per-feature entries with status island/stale enter the queue; the top-level prose lists are counted, not queued (:411-419).

läuftFINDING (Codex, CRITICAL 3) — cleanup resolves an attacker-influenced path, then falls back to unrestricted shutil.rmtree after git refuses; a junction to the primary checkout deletes the repo
The single most valuable output of the room, and the fix is real. worktree.py now owns the delete: check no-follow FIRST, never resolve before deciding, prove identity against a recorded allocation, raise on refusal (WorktreeContainmentError / WorktreeRemovalRace). The rewritten docstring goes further than Codex did and records three measured mechanisms, including one Codex did not name: CPython's shutil._rmtree_isdir reads st_reparse_tag from a stale scandir cache, so an already-enumerated sibling swapped for a junction mid-walk IS followed (measured 3/3 victims). The module states its own floor honestly: "this module is path hygiene, and path hygiene has a floor" — the race window is narrowed to microseconds, not closed.

läuftLede + sidecar abridgement (the never-silent distillation rule)
"NOTHING omitted means NO line: silence means 'you have it all'. A model that does not know it is reading an abridged view will reason confidently about the gap, so the gap is always labelled" (:29-33). If the sidecar write fails, the turn is still marked abridged with the failure named, because "naming a sidecar that does not exist would be worse than naming the failure" (:350). The sentence-boundary regex at :204 is built so that `daedalus/spine/attempt.py:295` and `1.5` are not cut in half.

läuftLESSON — a SECOND feedback loop: the summariser's own prompt was mirrored back into the session and queued for summarisation
The marker file records the refusal in its own words: status "skipped", reason "this module's own summarisation prompt, mirrored back into the session before the PROMPT_SENTINEL loop-breaker existed". Generalisable rule: any component that both reads and writes a shared transcript needs a self-recognition token, or it will eventually process its own output.

läuftLESSON — anchoring in completion-tuned models: given a transcript, they continue the strongest pattern in it, which is their own last turn
room.py:604-608 states the measurement, not the intuition: "observed three times, the same 21 characters returned verbatim, once with the full source of the file it had been asked about in front of it. Give this model class a task and material, never a room to echo." The solo prompt strips the transcript entirely and instructs the model not to repeat any sentence it was given. canary.py classifies anchoring as QUALITY, not LIVENESS, so a 7b model failing it does NOT trip the exit code (canary.py:32-40) — the distinction that keeps the transport alarm from being buried. Kaya's turn at room.md:342-349 is a live anchoring probe: a transcript of an answered question followed by a different question.
runs/council/room.py:604-608 (solo prompt mode) and :920-921 (solo defaults TRUE for the ollama lane); daedalus/council/canary.py:411-455 (anchoring probe)

läuftLESSON — feedback loop between a monitor and a mirror: the monitor's own notification arrives as a turn, which the mirror mirrors, which the monitor notices
stream_hook.py:177-180: "Mirroring these creates a feedback loop... Observed live, and it does not stop on its own." Six markers are filtered: <task-notification>, <system-reminder>, [SYSTEM NOTIFICATION, Monitor event:, <ide_opened_file>, <ide_selection>. Measured effect in runs/council/.stream_hook.log: six `skipped-plumbing` decisions between 13:18:41 and 13:23:57, one of them 9,751 chars. The log itself is a lesson — the hook is silent toward the session by design (a raising hook blocks the turn), so "a silent failure was indistinguishable from 'never ran'" (stream_hook.py:43-47) and every invocation writes one line saying what it decided.

läuftLESSON — prompt truncation through shell shims: a multi-line prompt does not survive an npm .cmd shim on Windows; it arrives cut at the first newline
The single most dangerous lesson in the file, and canary.py:8-13 says why: "The model answered 'your message was cut off', which reads as a model quirk rather than a transport bug." A liveness check asserting exit 0 or HTTP 200 is green through it. The arrival probe is built to detect exactly this — a nonce on the FIRST line, ~40 lines of filler, and the instruction plus a tail token at the END, so a prompt truncated anywhere fails deterministically. Kaya's turns at room.md:236-280 and :284-329 are two live arrival probes, both visibly trimmed at 4000 chars by the room.
runs/council/room.py:818-834 _exe (resolve .cmd/.exe/.bat, never the extensionless variant shutil.which finds first) and :841-846 (prompt on stdin, not argv); daedalus/council/canary.py:8-13 (incident) and :289-344 (arrival probe)
erreicht durch: The live symptom is room.md:30-32 — Codex's entire first turn is "It looks like your message was cut off after 'Everything below is…'. Please send the rest." Fix reached by ask_codex (room.py:836) and by `daedalus canary` arrival probe.
läuftPersisted vendor prompts (runs/council/.codex_prompt.txt, .agy_prompt.txt)
Cheap and load-bearing: because the prompt is on disk, "the model was sent a truncated prompt" is checkable after the fact rather than inferred from a confused reply. For agy the same file is scp'd to the RTX bench and read there via `agy -p "$(type ...)"` — the file IS the transport, which sidesteps the argv truncation class entirely.

läuftRoom attachment distillation (structcore-backed)
The caps encode a measured failure: "the slicer happily expands the neighbourhood to fill whatever budget it is handed, and the 'distilled' attach comes out BIGGER than the raw one -- measured, not hypothetical" (room.py:60-65). Files under 6k are sent whole because "a 'distillation' that grows the prompt is a lie". Tests assert distilled < raw (:161), that no file is ever inlined larger than its own body (:207), and that a tight budget truncates raw but fits distilled (:234).

läuftSession mirror hook (runs/council/stream_hook.py)
WIRED IN THE USER'S GLOBAL SETTINGS, NOT IN THE REPO — .claude/settings.json in the project does not mention it, so the wiring is neither version-controlled nor portable to another checkout or another machine. Design constraints (stream_hook.py:38-47): never fail the turn (every error path exits 0), skip empty and duplicate turns (Stop fires more than once around sub-agent activity), and log every decision because silence is indistinguishable from never running. Compression is mechanical string-boundary search, never a model (:34-36) — a model call in a synchronous hook would let a hung API hang the session.

läuftVendor canary (daedalus/council/canary.py) — four deterministic probes
The clearest artifact of the day: cross-vendor debugging turned into a permanent, cheap, repeatable check. Its docstring opens "WHY THIS EXISTS (the incident, 2026-07-28): Four vendor lanes were assumed healthy; two were silently broken, and both were found by accident while doing real work that then had to be thrown away." Rules: EVERY PROBE IS (prompt, checker) AND THE CHECKER IS DETERMINISTIC PYTHON — "No model judges another model's answer. A checker that needed an LLM would have exactly the failure mode the canary is supposed to catch." `unavailable` (agy not signed in, bench asleep) is separated from `failed` by construction and never trips the exit code. arrival/instruction are LIVENESS (alarm); anchoring/comprehension are QUALITY (reported loudly, no exit code). Also handles the egress point that offload.py's "ollama is local" justification depends on OLLAMA_HOST, an env var. Kaya's four unexplained turns in room.md (:236, :284, :333, :340, :353) are these probes running live through the room.
daedalus/council/canary.py:511 PROBES (arrival, instruction, anchoring, comprehension); `daedalus canary [--quick] [--vendors a,b] [--dry-run]` at cli.py:28, :625 _canary, :842 dispatch
erreicht durch: CLI. Tests: tests/test_council_canary.py (42 tests). Untracked (`?? daedalus/council/canary.py`) — built today, directly out of the room's incidents.

Measured, not inherited: 86 test files, 1399 tests collected (`python -m pytest --collect-only -q tests` → "1399 tests collected in 7.85s"). The HANDOFF figure "1223 passed, 35 subtests" is STALE — ~176 tests have landed since (council 190, spine 132, room/stream_hook 98, automint 11 are the bulk). There is NO conftest.py, NO pytest.ini, and NO [tool.pytest] section in pyproject.toml; discovery is plain rootdir-relative `tests/` collection, and there is no CI (`.github/workflows` does not exist) — the suite is run by hand. I found one live instance of exactly the pattern the owner asked about: the Python **lint gate is inert on this machine and no test can see it** (details below). kaputtPython lint gate (verifier._lint_py) — a defence that is currently inert
THIS IS THE 'GUARD SURVIVES ITS OWN DELETION' PATTERN, LIVE. `_lint_py` prefers ruff, falls back to pyflakes, and returns `(True, 'no linter (ruff/pyflakes) -- skipped')` when neither is installed — fail-open. Measured on this machine: `shutil.which('ruff')` is False and `import pyflakes` raises ImportError, so the gate currently passes EVERY file unconditionally. The only two tests that name it (tests/test_verify_gate.py:27 and :38) `patch('daedalus.verifier._lint_py', return_value=(False, ...))` — they mock the thing under test, so they prove only that verify() copies a boolean into `failed`. Deleting the entire ruff/pyflakes body would keep the suite green. Contrast test_era1_robustness.py:227, which runs the REAL `node --check` when node is on PATH (verified present) — the JS half of the same gate is honestly tested, the Python half is not.
